---
title: Benchmark Document (10000 lines)
author: RhoeMarkdown Benchmark Suite
date: 2026-04-01
---

### Key Metrics

- **Parsing throughput**: 50+ MB/s on Apple Silicon
- **Memory efficiency**: O(n) peak for streaming mode
- **Parallel speedup**: 3-8x on multi-core systems
- **SIMD acceleration**: 16-byte vectorized scanning
- **Cache locality**: SoA token layout optimization

```swift
let result = await RhoeMarkdownKit.parse(markdown)
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

1. Initialize the parser with default configuration
2. Load the document from the filesystem or network
3. Run Phase 1 Liquid preprocessing (if enabled)
4. Parse the preprocessed markdown into a raw AST
5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

### Architecture Overview

The [RhoeMarkdown](https://rhoe.dev) engine implements the **Rhoe language** specification (v4.0) with a [visitor-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visitor_pattern) AST architecture. All 52 canonical node kinds are supported, including *semantic blocks* (`!!!`), *visual blocks* (`:::`), and *composition directives* (`<< >>`).

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Implement visitor protocol infrastructure
- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases
- [x] Align inline naming to spec
- [ ] Implement incremental compilation
- [ ] Add GPU acceleration for rendering

The Rhoe markdown engine represents a significant advancement in document processing technology. By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

1. Initialize the parser with default configuration
2. Load the document from the filesystem or network
3. Run Phase 1 Liquid preprocessing (if enabled)
4. Parse the preprocessed markdown into a raw AST
5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

### Architecture Overview

The [RhoeMarkdown](https://rhoe.dev) engine implements the **Rhoe language** specification (v4.0) with a [visitor-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visitor_pattern) AST architecture. All 52 canonical node kinds are supported, including *semantic blocks* (`!!!`), *visual blocks* (`:::`), and *composition directives* (`<< >>`).

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Implement visitor protocol infrastructure
- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases
- [x] Align inline naming to spec
- [ ] Implement incremental compilation
- [ ] Add GPU acceleration for rendering

The Rhoe markdown engine represents a significant advancement in document processing technology. By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

## Section 108: Performance Analysis

This section covers the **performance characteristics** of the document processing engine. We analyze *throughput*, ~~latency~~, and `resource utilization` across different document sizes and configurations. The engine uses a 10-stage pipeline with structured concurrency for parallel processing.

### Key Metrics

- **Parsing throughput**: 50+ MB/s on Apple Silicon
- **Memory efficiency**: O(n) peak for streaming mode
- **Parallel speedup**: 3-8x on multi-core systems
- **SIMD acceleration**: 16-byte vectorized scanning
- **Cache locality**: SoA token layout optimization

```swift
let result = await RhoeMarkdownKit.parse(markdown)
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Implement visitor protocol infrastructure
- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases
- [x] Align inline naming to spec
- [ ] Implement incremental compilation
- [ ] Add GPU acceleration for rendering

The Rhoe markdown engine represents a significant advancement in document processing technology. By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

## Section 204: Performance Analysis

This section covers the **performance characteristics** of the document processing engine. We analyze *throughput*, ~~latency~~, and `resource utilization` across different document sizes and configurations. The engine uses a 10-stage pipeline with structured concurrency for parallel processing.

### Key Metrics

- **Parsing throughput**: 50+ MB/s on Apple Silicon
- **Memory efficiency**: O(n) peak for streaming mode
- **Parallel speedup**: 3-8x on multi-core systems
- **SIMD acceleration**: 16-byte vectorized scanning
- **Cache locality**: SoA token layout optimization

```swift
let result = await RhoeMarkdownKit.parse(markdown)
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

1. Initialize the parser with default configuration
2. Load the document from the filesystem or network
3. Run Phase 1 Liquid preprocessing (if enabled)
4. Parse the preprocessed markdown into a raw AST
5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

## Section 300: Performance Analysis

This section covers the **performance characteristics** of the document processing engine. We analyze *throughput*, ~~latency~~, and `resource utilization` across different document sizes and configurations. The engine uses a 10-stage pipeline with structured concurrency for parallel processing.

### Key Metrics

- **Parsing throughput**: 50+ MB/s on Apple Silicon
- **Memory efficiency**: O(n) peak for streaming mode
- **Parallel speedup**: 3-8x on multi-core systems
- **SIMD acceleration**: 16-byte vectorized scanning
- **Cache locality**: SoA token layout optimization


| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

All 52 canonical node kinds are supported, including *semantic blocks* (`!!!`), *visual blocks* (`:::`), and *composition directives* (`<< >>`).

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases

## Section 396: Performance Analysis

This section covers the **performance characteristics** of the document processing engine. We analyze *throughput*, ~~latency~~, and `resource utilization` across different document sizes and configurations. The engine uses a 10-stage pipeline with structured concurrency for parallel processing.

### Key Metrics

- **Parsing throughput**: 50+ MB/s on Apple Silicon
- **Memory efficiency**: O(n) peak for streaming mode
- **Parallel speedup**: 3-8x on multi-core systems
- **SIMD acceleration**: 16-byte vectorized scanning
- **Cache locality**: SoA token layout optimization

```swift
let result = await RhoeMarkdownKit.parse(markdown)
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

1. Initialize the parser with default configuration
2. Load the document from the filesystem or network
3. Run Phase 1 Liquid preprocessing (if enabled)
4. Parse the preprocessed markdown into a raw AST
5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

### Architecture Overview

The [RhoeMarkdown](https://rhoe.dev) engine implements the **Rhoe language** specification (v4.0) with a [visitor-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visitor_pattern) AST architecture. All 52 canonical node kinds are supported, including *semantic blocks* (`!!!`), *visual blocks* (`:::`), and *composition directives* (`<< >>`).

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Implement visitor protocol infrastructure
- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases
- [x] Align inline naming to spec
- [ ] Implement incremental compilation
- [ ] Add GPU acceleration for rendering

The Rhoe markdown engine represents a significant advancement in document processing technology. By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

```swift
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

1. Initialize the parser with default configuration
2. Load the document from the filesystem or network
3. Run Phase 1 Liquid preprocessing (if enabled)
4. Parse the preprocessed markdown into a raw AST
5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

### Architecture Overview

The [RhoeMarkdown](https://rhoe.dev) engine implements the **Rhoe language** specification (v4.0) with a [visitor-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visitor_pattern) AST architecture. All 52 canonical node kinds are supported, including *semantic blocks* (`!!!`), *visual blocks* (`:::`), and *composition directives* (`<< >>`).

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Implement visitor protocol infrastructure
- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases
- [x] Align inline naming to spec
- [ ] Implement incremental compilation
- [ ] Add GPU acceleration for rendering

The Rhoe markdown engine represents a significant advancement in document processing technology. By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

### Architecture Overview

The [RhoeMarkdown](https://rhoe.dev) engine implements the **Rhoe language** specification (v4.0) with a [visitor-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visitor_pattern) AST architecture. All 52 canonical node kinds are supported, including *semantic blocks* (`!!!`), *visual blocks* (`:::`), and *composition directives* (`<< >>`).

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Implement visitor protocol infrastructure
- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases
- [x] Align inline naming to spec
- [ ] Implement incremental compilation
- [ ] Add GPU acceleration for rendering

The Rhoe markdown engine represents a significant advancement in document processing technology. By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

## Section 600: Performance Analysis

This section covers the **performance characteristics** of the document processing engine. We analyze *throughput*, ~~latency~~, and `resource utilization` across different document sizes and configurations. The engine uses a 10-stage pipeline with structured concurrency for parallel processing.

### Key Metrics

- **Parsing throughput**: 50+ MB/s on Apple Silicon
- **Memory efficiency**: O(n) peak for streaming mode
- **Parallel speedup**: 3-8x on multi-core systems
- **SIMD acceleration**: 16-byte vectorized scanning
- **Cache locality**: SoA token layout optimization

```swift
let result = await RhoeMarkdownKit.parse(markdown)
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

2. Load the document from the filesystem or network
3. Run Phase 1 Liquid preprocessing (if enabled)
4. Parse the preprocessed markdown into a raw AST
5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

## Section 696: Performance Analysis

This section covers the **performance characteristics** of the document processing engine. We analyze *throughput*, ~~latency~~, and `resource utilization` across different document sizes and configurations. The engine uses a 10-stage pipeline with structured concurrency for parallel processing.

### Key Metrics

- **Parsing throughput**: 50+ MB/s on Apple Silicon
- **Memory efficiency**: O(n) peak for streaming mode
- **Parallel speedup**: 3-8x on multi-core systems
- **SIMD acceleration**: 16-byte vectorized scanning
- **Cache locality**: SoA token layout optimization

```swift
let result = await RhoeMarkdownKit.parse(markdown)
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.

> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

## Section 792: Performance Analysis

This section covers the **performance characteristics** of the document processing engine. We analyze *throughput*, ~~latency~~, and `resource utilization` across different document sizes and configurations. The engine uses a 10-stage pipeline with structured concurrency for parallel processing.

### Key Metrics

- **Parsing throughput**: 50+ MB/s on Apple Silicon
- **Memory efficiency**: O(n) peak for streaming mode
- **Parallel speedup**: 3-8x on multi-core systems
- **SIMD acceleration**: 16-byte vectorized scanning
- **Cache locality**: SoA token layout optimization

```swift
let result = await RhoeMarkdownKit.parse(markdown)
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

1. Initialize the parser with default configuration
2. Load the document from the filesystem or network
3. Run Phase 1 Liquid preprocessing (if enabled)
4. Parse the preprocessed markdown into a raw AST
5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

### Architecture Overview

The [RhoeMarkdown](https://rhoe.dev) engine implements the **Rhoe language** specification (v4.0) with a [visitor-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visitor_pattern) AST architecture. All 52 canonical node kinds are supported, including *semantic blocks* (`!!!`), *visual blocks* (`:::`), and *composition directives* (`<< >>`).

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Implement visitor protocol infrastructure
- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases
- [x] Align inline naming to spec
- [ ] Implement incremental compilation
- [ ] Add GPU acceleration for rendering

The Rhoe markdown engine represents a significant advancement in document processing technology. By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

```swift
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

1. Initialize the parser with default configuration
2. Load the document from the filesystem or network
3. Run Phase 1 Liquid preprocessing (if enabled)
4. Parse the preprocessed markdown into a raw AST
5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

### Architecture Overview

The [RhoeMarkdown](https://rhoe.dev) engine implements the **Rhoe language** specification (v4.0) with a [visitor-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visitor_pattern) AST architecture. All 52 canonical node kinds are supported, including *semantic blocks* (`!!!`), *visual blocks* (`:::`), and *composition directives* (`<< >>`).

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Implement visitor protocol infrastructure
- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases
- [x] Align inline naming to spec
- [ ] Implement incremental compilation
- [ ] Add GPU acceleration for rendering

The Rhoe markdown engine represents a significant advancement in document processing technology. By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

4. Parse the preprocessed markdown into a raw AST
5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

### Architecture Overview

The [RhoeMarkdown](https://rhoe.dev) engine implements the **Rhoe language** specification (v4.0) with a [visitor-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visitor_pattern) AST architecture. All 52 canonical node kinds are supported, including *semantic blocks* (`!!!`), *visual blocks* (`:::`), and *composition directives* (`<< >>`).

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Implement visitor protocol infrastructure
- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases
- [x] Align inline naming to spec
- [ ] Implement incremental compilation
- [ ] Add GPU acceleration for rendering

The Rhoe markdown engine represents a significant advancement in document processing technology. By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

## Section 996: Performance Analysis

This section covers the **performance characteristics** of the document processing engine. We analyze *throughput*, ~~latency~~, and `resource utilization` across different document sizes and configurations. The engine uses a 10-stage pipeline with structured concurrency for parallel processing.

### Key Metrics

- **Parsing throughput**: 50+ MB/s on Apple Silicon
- **Memory efficiency**: O(n) peak for streaming mode
- **Parallel speedup**: 3-8x on multi-core systems
- **SIMD acceleration**: 16-byte vectorized scanning
- **Cache locality**: SoA token layout optimization

```swift
let result = await RhoeMarkdownKit.parse(markdown)
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

## Section 1092: Performance Analysis

This section covers the **performance characteristics** of the document processing engine. We analyze *throughput*, ~~latency~~, and `resource utilization` across different document sizes and configurations. The engine uses a 10-stage pipeline with structured concurrency for parallel processing.

### Key Metrics

- **Parsing throughput**: 50+ MB/s on Apple Silicon
- **Memory efficiency**: O(n) peak for streaming mode
- **Parallel speedup**: 3-8x on multi-core systems
- **SIMD acceleration**: 16-byte vectorized scanning
- **Cache locality**: SoA token layout optimization

```swift
let result = await RhoeMarkdownKit.parse(markdown)
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**

By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

## Section 1188: Performance Analysis

This section covers the **performance characteristics** of the document processing engine. We analyze *throughput*, ~~latency~~, and `resource utilization` across different document sizes and configurations. The engine uses a 10-stage pipeline with structured concurrency for parallel processing.

### Key Metrics

- **Parsing throughput**: 50+ MB/s on Apple Silicon
- **Memory efficiency**: O(n) peak for streaming mode
- **Parallel speedup**: 3-8x on multi-core systems
- **SIMD acceleration**: 16-byte vectorized scanning
- **Cache locality**: SoA token layout optimization

```swift
let result = await RhoeMarkdownKit.parse(markdown)
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

1. Initialize the parser with default configuration
2. Load the document from the filesystem or network
3. Run Phase 1 Liquid preprocessing (if enabled)
4. Parse the preprocessed markdown into a raw AST
5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

### Architecture Overview

The [RhoeMarkdown](https://rhoe.dev) engine implements the **Rhoe language** specification (v4.0) with a [visitor-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visitor_pattern) AST architecture. All 52 canonical node kinds are supported, including *semantic blocks* (`!!!`), *visual blocks* (`:::`), and *composition directives* (`<< >>`).

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Implement visitor protocol infrastructure
- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases
- [x] Align inline naming to spec
- [ ] Implement incremental compilation
- [ ] Add GPU acceleration for rendering

The Rhoe markdown engine represents a significant advancement in document processing technology. By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

```swift
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

1. Initialize the parser with default configuration
2. Load the document from the filesystem or network
3. Run Phase 1 Liquid preprocessing (if enabled)
4. Parse the preprocessed markdown into a raw AST
5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

### Architecture Overview

The [RhoeMarkdown](https://rhoe.dev) engine implements the **Rhoe language** specification (v4.0) with a [visitor-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visitor_pattern) AST architecture. All 52 canonical node kinds are supported, including *semantic blocks* (`!!!`), *visual blocks* (`:::`), and *composition directives* (`<< >>`).

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Implement visitor protocol infrastructure
- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases
- [x] Align inline naming to spec
- [ ] Implement incremental compilation
- [ ] Add GPU acceleration for rendering

The Rhoe markdown engine represents a significant advancement in document processing technology. By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

3. Run Phase 1 Liquid preprocessing (if enabled)
4. Parse the preprocessed markdown into a raw AST
5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

### Architecture Overview

The [RhoeMarkdown](https://rhoe.dev) engine implements the **Rhoe language** specification (v4.0) with a [visitor-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visitor_pattern) AST architecture. All 52 canonical node kinds are supported, including *semantic blocks* (`!!!`), *visual blocks* (`:::`), and *composition directives* (`<< >>`).

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Implement visitor protocol infrastructure
- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases
- [x] Align inline naming to spec
- [ ] Implement incremental compilation
- [ ] Add GPU acceleration for rendering

The Rhoe markdown engine represents a significant advancement in document processing technology. By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

## Section 1392: Performance Analysis

This section covers the **performance characteristics** of the document processing engine. We analyze *throughput*, ~~latency~~, and `resource utilization` across different document sizes and configurations. The engine uses a 10-stage pipeline with structured concurrency for parallel processing.

### Key Metrics

- **Parsing throughput**: 50+ MB/s on Apple Silicon
- **Memory efficiency**: O(n) peak for streaming mode
- **Parallel speedup**: 3-8x on multi-core systems
- **SIMD acceleration**: 16-byte vectorized scanning
- **Cache locality**: SoA token layout optimization

```swift
let result = await RhoeMarkdownKit.parse(markdown)
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**

The Rhoe markdown engine represents a significant advancement in document processing technology. By combining a principled language specification (the Ring 0/1/2 architecture) with a high-performance compiler implementation in Swift 6, the engine achieves throughput levels previously only seen in purpose-built C parsers. The visitor-based AST dispatch pattern eliminates the traditional maintenance burden of exhaustive switch statements, while the 10-stage pipeline provides clean separation of concerns between parsing, normalization, and rendering.

## Section 1488: Performance Analysis

This section covers the **performance characteristics** of the document processing engine. We analyze *throughput*, ~~latency~~, and `resource utilization` across different document sizes and configurations. The engine uses a 10-stage pipeline with structured concurrency for parallel processing.

### Key Metrics

- **Parsing throughput**: 50+ MB/s on Apple Silicon
- **Memory efficiency**: O(n) peak for streaming mode
- **Parallel speedup**: 3-8x on multi-core systems
- **SIMD acceleration**: 16-byte vectorized scanning
- **Cache locality**: SoA token layout optimization

```swift
let result = await RhoeMarkdownKit.parse(markdown)
let html = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderHTML(result.document)
let latex = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderLaTeX(result.document)
let typst = RhoeMarkdownKit.renderTypst(result.document)
print("Parsed \(result.document.blocks.count) blocks")
```

| Metric | Value | Unit |
|--------|-------|------|
| Parse time | 12.5 | ms |
| Token count | 45,230 | tokens |
| Block count | 1,247 | blocks |
| Memory peak | 8.3 | MB |
| Throughput | 52.7 | MB/s |

> **Note:** The benchmark results shown here were collected on Apple Silicon
> hardware (M2 Pro) with the engine compiled in release mode. Debug builds
> are approximately 5x slower due to optimization flags and safety checks.
>
> — *RhoeMarkdown Engineering Team*

1. Initialize the parser with default configuration
2. Load the document from the filesystem or network
3. Run Phase 1 Liquid preprocessing (if enabled)
4. Parse the preprocessed markdown into a raw AST
5. Execute the 10-stage normalization pipeline
6. Render to the target format (HTML, LaTeX, Typst)

### Architecture Overview

The [RhoeMarkdown](https://rhoe.dev) engine implements the **Rhoe language** specification (v4.0) with a [visitor-based](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visitor_pattern) AST architecture. All 52 canonical node kinds are supported, including *semantic blocks* (`!!!`), *visual blocks* (`:::`), and *composition directives* (`<< >>`).

- **Block nodes** (33 canonical kinds)
  - Structural: Section, Paragraph, BlockQuote, List
  - Semantic: Admonition, FormalBlock, SpeakerNotes
  - Media: Figure, Image, DiagramBlock, Shape
  - Data: Table, TableHead, TableBody, TableRow
- **Inline nodes** (19 canonical kinds)
  - Markup: Emphasis, Strong, Strikethrough, Highlight
  - References: Citation, CrossReference, FootnoteRef
  - Code: CodeSpan, InlineMath, RawInline

The processing time complexity is:

$$T(n) = O(n \cdot \log(n)) + O(k \cdot p)$$

where *n* is the document size, *k* is the number of pipeline passes, and *p* is the average pass complexity.

**Parsing**
: The process of converting source text into an Abstract Syntax Tree (AST).

**Rendering**
: The process of converting an AST into a target format like HTML or LaTeX.

- [x] Migrate all writers to visitor dispatch
- [x] Add 19 missing block cases
- [x] Align inline naming to spec

- [ ] Add GPU acceleration for rendering
